# The joint Dickman law for consecutive integers

Coordinate `EN-101-000-0022-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M1-B-ML-H-0034` | `PM-012.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `The-joint-Dickman-law-for-consecutive-integers-September-24-2026` | date 2026-09-24 | derived from the openai/math preprint The-joint-Dickman-law-for-consecutive-integers-September-24-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "Let $`P^+(n)`$ denote the largest prime factor of <i>n</i>. We prove that $`\\log P^+(n)/\\log n`$ and $`\\log P^+(n+1)/\\log n`$ are asymptotically independent in ordinary natural density, with Dickman marginals. This resolves the Erdős–Pomerance joint Dickman conjecture positively and implies that the ordering $`P^+(n)\\lt P^+(n+1)`$ has natural density 1/2.",
  "canonical_anchor": "pm-s7",
  "claim_status": "CORPUS_CLAIMED",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let $`P^+(n)`$ denote the largest prime factor of <i>n</i>. We prove that $`\log P^+(n)/\log n`$ and $`\log P^+(n+1)/\log n`$ are asymptotically independent in ordinary natural density, with Dickman marginals. This resolves the Erdős–Pomerance joint Dickman conjecture positively and implies that the ordering $`P^+(n)\lt P^+(n+1)`$ has natural density 1/2.

- **claim-joint-dickman-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every fixed $a,b\in(0,1)$, $$\lim_{X\to\infty}\frac1X \#\{2\leq n\leq X:P^+(n)\leq n^a, \ P^+(n+1)\leq n^b\} =\rho(1/a)\rho(1/b).$$ Here the limit is taken over all real $X$, with ordinary, unweighted counting.
  - Explanation: The normalized logarithms of the largest prime factors of n and n+1 are asymptotically independent in ordinary natural density, each with the Dickman marginal.
- **claim-ordering-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-comparison)): One has $$\lim_{X\to\infty}\frac1X \#\{2\leq n\leq X:P^+(n)<P^+(n+1)\}=\frac12.$$ The reverse ordering has the same natural density.
  - Explanation: The ordering of the largest prime factors of consecutive integers has natural density one half in both directions, because the limiting law has no atoms.
- **claim-label-marginal-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-labels-marginal)): For every fixed $0<\alpha<\beta<\infty$, positive integer $q$, and residue class $a\pmod q$, the distribution of $$\bigl(\Omega_{\mathcal B_{k,x}}(n)\bigr)_{1\leq k<J}$$ among the integers $\alpha x<n\leq\beta x$, $n\equiv a\pmod q$, normalized to have mass one, converges as $x\to\infty$. Its limit depends only on $J$, and not on $\alpha,\beta,q,a$.* *Consequently there is a number $\mu=\mu(J,\zeta_1,\ldots,\zeta_{J-1})$ with $\|\mu\|\leq1$ such that $$\label{eq:labels-marginal-mean} \lim_{x\to\infty} \frac{q}{(\beta-\alpha)x} \sum_{\substack{\alpha x<n\leq\beta x\\n\equiv a\pmod q}}f_x(n) =\mu.$$ The same assertion holds for $g_x$, with a mean $\mu_g$ depending only on $J$ and the vector $(\xi_k)_{k=1}^{J-1}$. Write $$\label{eq:labels-centered} F_x(n)=f_x(n)-\mu.$$ Then $\|F_x(n)\|\leq2$, and for every fixed positive integer $u$, $$\label{eq:labels-multiplier} F_x(un)=F_x(n),\qquad g_x(un)=g_x(n)\qquad(n\geq1)$$ for all sufficiently large $x$ in terms of $u,J$.
  - Explanation: Bin count vectors converge in distribution along progressions to a limit depending only on the number of bins, giving label means and invariance under fixed multipliers.
- **claim-mixed-decorrelation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-mixed)): For every fixed integer $J\geq2$ and every pair of fixed phase vectors $(\zeta_k)_{k=1}^{J-1}$ and $(\xi_k)_{k=1}^{J-1}$ of absolute value one, the corresponding labels satisfy $$\label{eq:mixed} \lim_{x\to\infty}\frac1x\sum_{1\leq n<x} \overline{g_x(n)}F_x(n+1)=0,$$ where $F_x=f_x-\mu$ and the limit is through all integer scales.
  - Explanation: The centered label at n+1 is uncorrelated with the unit-modulus label at n, for arbitrary phase vectors.
- **claim-weighted-short-averages** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-labels-short)): Let $T=T(B)\to\infty$, let $L_B$ be positive integers tending to infinity, and let $a_B$ be any integer for each $B$. Fix $0<s_0<s_1<\infty$, a positive integer $D$, a residue $r\pmod D$, and $v\geq0$. Then $$\label{eq:labels-short} \lim_{B\to\infty}\limsup_{x\to\infty} \frac1{Tx}\sum_{s_0Tx\leq n\leq s_1Tx} \left\| \frac1{L_B}\sum_{i=a_B+1}^{a_B+L_B} \mathbf 1_{i\equiv r\ (D)}F_x(n+i)G_{B,v}(n+i) \right\|^2=0.$$ For each fixed $B$ all the arguments of the arithmetic functions are positive once $x$ is sufficiently large. The same conclusion holds if the inner upper limit is taken along any sequence $x\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: Mean-square short averages of the centered label weighted by fair-split factors vanish when x tends to infinity before B.
- **claim-real-short-interval** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-labels-real-mr)): There is a function $r(H)$ tending to zero as $H\to\infty$ such that, for every real multiplicative function $g:\mathbb N\to[-1,1]$ and $2\leq H\leq X$, $$\label{eq:labels-real-MR} \frac1X\int_X^{2X} \|\mathcal A_Hg(z)-\mathcal L_Xg\|^2\,dz\leq r(H).$$
  - Explanation: Short averages of real bounded multiplicative functions match long averages in mean square, with error depending only on interval length.
- **claim-complex-short-interval** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-labels-complex-mrt)): For every multiplicative function $g:\mathbb N\to\mathbb C$ with $\|g\|\leq1$ and $X\geq H\geq10$, $$\label{eq:labels-complex-quantitative} \frac1X\int_X^{2X}\|\mathcal A_Hg(z)\|^2\,dz \ll (1+M(g;X))e^{-M(g;X)} +\left(\frac{\log\log H}{\log H}\right)^2 +(\log X)^{-1/50},$$ with an absolute implied constant.
  - Explanation: Short averages of complex 1-bounded multiplicative functions are controlled by the pretentious distance minimum.
- **claim-character-distance-divergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-labels-character-distance)): Fix $J\geq2$, positive constants $c,C$, a nonprincipal Dirichlet character $\chi$ of modulus $q_\chi$, and a finite set $E$ of primes. Suppose that, for each $x$, $h_x$ is a 1-bounded multiplicative function satisfying $$h_x(p)=\chi(p)\qquad(p\leq x^{1/J},\ p\notin E).$$ With $X=cx$, one has $$\label{eq:labels-character-distance} \inf_{\|t\|\leq CX}\mathbb D(h_x,n^{it};X)^2\longrightarrow\infty \qquad(x\to\infty).$$ The assertion is uniform over all the functions $h_x$ with this prime agreement. The threshold for $x$ may depend on $J,c,C,\chi,E$.
  - Explanation: Functions agreeing with a nonprincipal character on small primes stay far from every twist, uniformly.
- **claim-classical-selberg-delange** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-arith-classical-sd)): For each fixed nonnegative integer $H$ and either of the above values of $z$, there are real constants $c_j(z)$ such that, for $Y\ge3$, $$\sum_{n\le Y}g_z(n) =Y\sum_{j=0}^{H}c_j(z)(\log Y)^{z-1-j} +O_H\bigl(Y(\log Y)^{z-2-H}\bigr),$$ where $$c_0(z)=\frac1{\Gamma(z)} \prod_p(1+z/p)(1-1/p)^z>0.$$ For each fixed $C,D>0$, uniformly over nonprincipal Dirichlet characters $\chi$ of modulus $d\le(\log Y)^C$, one has $$\sum_{n\le Y}g_z(n)\chi(n)\ll_{C,D}Y(\log Y)^{-D}.$$ The constants in the second estimate, and the threshold after which it holds, need not be effective.
  - Explanation: Classical Selberg-Delange expansion for the weighted squarefree sum, with power saving under nonprincipal character twists.
- **claim-rough-selberg-delange** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-arith-rough-sd)): Fix $D>0$. There is a fixed integer $H=H(D)$ and real coefficients $C_{j,z}(P_0)$, $0\le j\le H$, such that uniformly for $B^{0.89}\le\log Y\le4B$, $$\sum_{n\le Y}g_{z,P_0}(n) =Y\sum_{j=0}^{H}C_{j,z}(P_0)(\log Y)^{z-1-j} +O_D(YB^{-D}).$$ There is an absolute constant $C_0$ such that for every fixed $j$, $$\|C_{j,z}(P_0)\|\ll_j(\log P_0)^{C_0+j},\qquad C_{0,z}(P_0)=c_0(z)\prod_{p\le P_0}(1+z/p)^{-1} \sim\frac{e^{-\gamma_{\rm E}z}}{\Gamma(z)}(\log P_0)^{-z},$$ where $\gamma_{\rm E}$ is Euler’s constant. Uniformly for nonprincipal $\chi$ of modulus $d\le B^{15}$ in the same size range, $$\sum_{n\le Y}g_{z,P_0}(n)\chi(n)\ll_D YB^{-D}.$$
  - Explanation: The expansion persists for rough integers with a moving cutoff, with uniform polynomial saving in B.
- **claim-local-coefficient-laws** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-arith-local-laws)): Let $w_3$ be smooth and supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\infty)$. Uniformly for $0.9B\le\log X\le2.2B$, $q\le B^{15}$, $(u,q)=1$, and real $\xi$ with $\|\xi\|\le B^{14}$, $$\sum_c A_0(c)w_3(c/X)e(cu/q+\xi c/X) =X\frac{\mu_{\rm Mob}(q)}{\varphi(q)} \int w_3(t)m_B(\log(Xt)/B)e(\xi t)\,dt +O(XB^{-50}). \label{eq:1}$$ The convention $q=1$ is included. The constant in the error is controlled by a fixed constant times finitely many low-order smooth norms of $w_3$; the expansion order defining $m_B$ is independent of $w_3$.* *Let $\nu_z$ be the law of the product of primes of $\mathcal P$ selected independently with probabilities $z/p$, and write $s=\log a/B$ for its log coordinate. For $d\le B^{15}$, every such product is a unit modulo $d$. Uniformly for all intervals $I'\subset[B^{-1/10},3.2]$ and unit residues $r\pmod d$, $$\mathbb P_{\nu_z}(s\in I',\ a\equiv r\pmod d) =\frac1{\varphi(d)}\int_{I'}f_{z,B}(s)\,ds+O(B^{-80}). \label{eq:2}$$ Uniformly for $0\le\delta\le1$, $$\mathbb P_{\nu_z}(s\le\delta)\ll(\delta+1/R)^z. \label{eq:3}$$ For each positive integer $n\le\exp(3.2B)$, $$\nu_{1/2}(n)\ll\frac{A_0(n)}{Bn}, \label{eq:4}$$ where both sides are zero unless $n$ is a squarefree rough product, apart from the allowed empty product $n=1$.
  - Explanation: Local asymptotics for the coefficient weight with additive phases and local densities for random prime products.
- **claim-weighted-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-arith-weighted-sieve)): There is a sufficiently small $c_k>0$, depending only on fixed $k$, with the following properties for $Z\ge2$.* *In an interval of length $N\ge1$, at most $k$ forbidden residues at each prime $p\le Z$ give an upper bound $$C_kN\prod_{p\le Z}(1-\theta_p)+O_k(N^{0.8}) \qquad(Z\le N^{c_k}).$$ In a rectangle of side lengths $N_1,N_2\ge1$, suppose that at each prime the forbidden set is a union of at most $k$ proper affine lines. With $N_* =\min(N_1,N_2)$, there is the corresponding bound $$C_kN_1N_2\prod_{p\le Z}(1-\theta_p) +O_k(N_1N_2N_*^{-0.4}) \qquad(Z\le N_*^{c_k}).$$ An application may omit specified primes from the restrictions, provided it also omits their factors from the products.* *Both assertions also apply to reducing local weights. At each prime, attach a factor in $[0,1]$ to each of at most $k$ specified residue classes modulo $p$ in the interval case, or at most $k$ proper affine lines in the rectangle case. The factor is applied on its class or line and equals one off it. Replace $1-\theta_p$ by the residue average of the product of these factors.
  - Explanation: Upper sieve bounds on intervals and rectangles, including reducing local weights.
- **claim-coefficient-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-arith-coefficient-moments)): On these intervals, $$X^{-1}\sum_{n\asymp X}A_0(n)\ll1,\qquad X^{-1}\sum_{n\asymp X}A_0(n)^2\ll B^{1/4+o(1)}. \label{eq:5}$$
  - Explanation: First moment of the coefficient weight is bounded; second moment is a small power of B.
- **claim-form-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-arith-form-bounds)): Uniformly for $0<\|j\|\le T$ and squarefree rough $b\asymp TX$, $$\sum_{\substack{c\asymp X\\b+jc\asymp TX,\ b+jc>0}} A_0(c)A_0(b+jc)\ll X\Sigma(j). \label{eq:6}$$ On the same fixed log-size ranges, $$\sum_{\substack{b\asymp TX,\ c\asymp X\\a=b+jc\asymp TX,\ a>0}} A_0(b)A_0(a)A_0(c)\ll TX^2\Sigma(j). \label{eq:7}$$ The implied constants do not depend on $b,j,X$, or $B$.
  - Explanation: Two- and three-form coefficient sums along shifts are bounded with a divisor-type loss in the shift.
- **claim-regularity-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-arith-regularity-loss)): There is an absolute $c_3>0$ such that, for each fixed choice of the grid, tolerance, and compact log-size ranges above, there are $C>0$ and a function $\epsilon_B\to0$ such that the part of the untruncated sum in <a href="#eq:7" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:7">[eq:7]</a> where at least one coefficient is not regular is at most $$C TX^2\Sigma(j)\bigl(\epsilon_B+e^{-c_3C_*}\bigr). \label{eq:9}$$ The constant $C$ may depend on the fixed grid, tolerance, and scale ranges, but not on $C_*,j,X,B$. The function $\epsilon_B$ may depend on the same fixed data but is independent of $C_*$. The analogous bound with scale $X$ applies to the first-moment sum of $A_0$ in <a href="#eq:5" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:5">[eq:5]</a>. For the product of two independent coefficient weights in a box, it applies with the corresponding area in place of $TX^2\Sigma(j)$.* *The same upper probability $O(\epsilon_B+e^{-c_3C_*})$ holds for failure of regularity in a set of independent prime indicators on $\mathcal P$ with parameters $1/(2p)+O(p^{-2})$, uniformly when the constant in the $O$ term is fixed. It also holds after omitting a deterministic subset $E_B\subset\mathcal P$ satisfying $$\sum_{p\in E_B}\frac1p\le C_E\frac{B}{P_0}$$ for a fixed $C_E>0$. The subset may depend on $B$, and the bound is uniform over all such subsets for each fixed $C_E$.
  - Explanation: Non-regular configurations carry negligible mass, up to a vanishing term and an exponentially small term in the tail parameter.
- **claim-divisor-weight-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-amp-divisor-moments)): There exist $C_0>0$ and $d_0>0$ such that, for each fixed $C_*\ge C_0$ and all sufficiently large $B$, $$\label{eq:12} \\|D_B\\|_{L^2(\widehat{\mathbb Z})}\ll_{C_*}1, \qquad \int_{\widehat{\mathbb Z}}D_B(w)\,dw\ge d_0.$$ The constant $d_0$ is independent of $C_*$ and $B$. In addition, $\int D_B\ll1$ with a constant independent of $C_*$.
  - Explanation: The divisor weight has bounded second moment and mean bounded below.
- **claim-site-model-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-amp-site-model)): For each fixed $C_*\ge0$, as $B\to\infty$, $$\int_{\widehat{\mathbb Z}}D_B(w)^r\,dw=\mathbb E\widetilde D_B^r+o(1) \qquad(r=1,2).$$
  - Explanation: First and second moments of the divisor weight equal those of an independent fair-split model up to a vanishing error.
- **claim-addition-small-ball** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-amp-small-ball)): Fix $H>0$. Write $L=\log P_0$. For sufficiently large $B$, let $L\le Y\le8B$, put $U=\min(Y,4B)$, and form a random product $Q_Y$ by including each prime $P_0<p\le e^U$ independently with probability $1/(4p)$. Uniformly over all intervals $J\subset\mathbb R$ of length at most $H$, $$\mathbb P\bigl(\log Q_Y\in J\cap[Y/2,\infty)\bigr) \ll_H\frac1Y.$$ The implicit constant is independent of $B$, $Y$, and $J$.
  - Explanation: A random prime product rarely lands in a short logarithmic window above half its scale.
- **claim-profile-preservation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-preservation)): For each fixed $C_*\ge C_0$, put $H(t,w)=\phi(t)W(t,w)$. Then $$\label{eq:amp-preservation} \int H(t,w)D_B(w)\,dt\,dw -\left(\int H\,dt\,dw\right)\left(\int D_B\,dw\right) \longrightarrow0\qquad(B\to\infty).$$
  - Explanation: The profile correlation against the divisor weight factorizes asymptotically into the product of means.
- **claim-amplified-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-amp-energy)): There is $c_5>0$, independent of all sufficiently large fixed $C_*$, for which the nonnegative energy below satisfies the displayed lower bound: $$\label{eq:15} \begin{gathered} I_{2,x} = \frac1x\sum_{c\ge1} \rho_0(\log c/B)A(c) \sum_{m\ge1}K(m) \left\| \sum_{\substack{a\ge1\\c\mid am+1}} A(a)K(l_a)\psi(a/(Tc)) \phi(am/x)F_x(am+1) \right\|^2, \\ \liminf_{B\to\infty}\ \liminf_{x\to\infty} \frac{I_{2,x}}{BT}\ge c_5 \end{gathered}$$ Its diagonal contribution is negligible: $$\lim_{B\to\infty}\ \limsup_{x\to\infty} \frac{I_{2,x}^{\mathrm{diag}}}{BT}=0.$$ Here $I_{2,x}^{\mathrm{diag}}$ denotes the terms with equal coefficient indices on expanding the square, and $l_a=(am+1)/c$ as in <a href="#eq:10" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:10">[eq:10]</a>.
  - Explanation: The quadratic energy has a positive uniform lower bound while its diagonal part vanishes.
- **claim-edge-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-graph-edge-mass)): Uniformly for $0<\|j\|\le T$ and $s\in\mathcal S$, $$\label{eq:18} \mathbb E_{u\in\widehat{\mathbb Z}}\mathcal K_j(u,s)\ll\frac{\Sigma(j)}T.$$ The implied constant can be independent of the regularity parameter $C_*$.
  - Explanation: Mean edge mass of the arithmetic kernel is bounded by the shift loss divided by T.
- **claim-row-mean** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-graph-row-mean)): For every possible endpoint type $S_i$, not merely almost every typical type, and every allowed lag $j=k-i$, $$\label{eq:23} \mathbb E_{S_k}\mathcal L_{ik}(S_i,S_k;s) \ll\frac{\Sigma(j)}T.$$ In particular, every conditional expected degree is $O(1)$, and $\mathbb E\sum_{i,k}\mathcal L_{ik}=O(M)$.
  - Explanation: The conditional expected row of the latent kernel is bounded for every endpoint type.
- **claim-root-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-graph-root-comparison)): Let $\mathcal M_{ik}(S_i,S_k;s)$ be a symmetric real kernel, zero on nonpairs, with total absolute mass $O(B^{10})$ uniformly in its arguments. Then $$\label{eq:21} \begin{split} &\mathbb E_u\left\\| \bigl(\mathcal K_{k-i}(u+i,s) -\mathcal M_{ik}(S_i(u),S_k(u);s)\bigr)_{i,k} \right\\|_\square\\ &\hspace{15pt}\le o(1)+ \mathbb E_{(S_i)\,\mathrm{indep}} \left\\|\bigl(\mathcal L_{ik}(S_i,S_k;s) -\mathcal M_{ik}(S_i,S_k;s)\bigr)_{i,k}\right\\|_\square. \end{split}$$ The error is uniform for $s\in\mathcal S$.
  - Explanation: The arithmetic kernel can be compared with the independent-site latent kernel in expected cut norm.
- **claim-row-second-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-moments-rows)): For a sufficiently fine fixed regularity grid and then a sufficiently small fixed tolerance, the independent-site latent kernel satisfies $$\sum_{k\ne i}\mathbb E\,\mathcal L_{ik}^{2}\ll B^{-.21} \qquad (1\le i\le M). \label{eq:24}$$ Moreover, its normalized total mass has bounded second moment: $$\mathbb E\left(\frac1M\sum_{i,k}\mathcal L_{ik}\right)^2\ll1.$$ For every set $I\subseteq\{1,\ldots,M\}$, the same estimates hold after replacing $\mathcal L_{ik}$ by $\mathbf 1_{\{i,k\in I\}}\mathcal L_{ik}$; the normalization remains $M$.
  - Explanation: Row second moments of the latent kernel have a power saving; total mass has bounded second moment.
- **claim-representation-multiplicity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-moments-multiplicity)): The grid and tolerance can be chosen so that, uniformly for $(a,b,c)\in\mathcal C_j$ and $\eta T\le \|j\|\le T$, $$\mathbb E^{\mathrm{tilt}}_{a,b} \big[ \mathbf 1_{\mathcal T_b\ \mathrm{regular}} \mathbf 1_{\mathcal T_a\ \mathrm{regular}} N_{a,b,c}(\mathcal T_b,\mathcal T_a) \big] \ll B^{.009}.$$
  - Explanation: Weighted multiplicity of alternative representations is a small power of B.
- **claim-channel-operators** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-channels-operators)): For every fixed coarse partition, uniformly for $1\le d\le B$, $$\label{eq:31} \\|U_d\\|\ll 1,\qquad \\|(1-P_{\rm av})U_d\\|\ll B^{-c_7}, \qquad c_7=\frac1{200}.$$ Moreover, for every $\epsilon_1>0$, one can choose $m_1$ such that $$\label{eq:32} \limsup_{B\to\infty}\ \sup_{\\|g\\|_2\le1}\\|u_g-P_{m_1}u_g\\|_2\le\epsilon_1 .$$ All constants are independent of the input $g$. The sufficiently large threshold for $B$ may depend on the chosen fixed partition.
  - Explanation: The fair-split channel is bounded, kills nonconstant residue modes, and is uniformly compact on a coarse partition.
- **claim-two-splits-coupling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-channels-two-splits)): Let $b_1,b_2$ be two conditionally independent fair splits of the same site. Their joint law can be coupled, with failure probability $O(P_0^{-1})$, to $$(CE_1,CE_2),$$ where $C,E_1,E_2$ are independent products with law $\nu_{1/4}$. Replacing the two-split step kernel by this independent-product step kernel changes its operator norm by $O(B^5/P_0)$, uniformly for $d\le B$.
  - Explanation: Two fair splits of one site couple to independent common and exclusive products with negligible operator change.
- **claim-low-exclusive** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-channels-low-exclusive)): For $0<\theta\le1/10$, the contribution to $K_d^{\rm ind}$ from $\ell_B(E_i)\le\theta$, for either $i=1$ or $i=2$, has operator norm $$\ll (\theta+1/R)^{1/4}+B^{-70}+B^5/P_0,$$ uniformly for $d\le B$. The same bound, with a different absolute constant, holds for the union of the two events.
  - Explanation: Small exclusive products contribute little in operator norm, via a two-sided Schur estimate.
- **claim-coarse-features** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-channels-features)): For the chosen coarse partition, define $$V_l(S)=\frac1{\|I_l\|} \mathbb P_{\rm split}(x_b\in I_l\mid S).$$ Then $0\le V_l\le \|I_l\|^{-1}$, and for every $g\in L^2(\Omega_B)$ the coarse value of $P_{m_1}u_g$ on $I_l$ is exactly $$\mathbb E[g(S)V_l(S)].$$ In particular the bounds <a href="#eq:31" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:31">[eq:31]</a>–<a href="#eq:32" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:32">[eq:32]</a> and this identity hold uniformly when $g$ varies with a position, with $B$, or with an external parameter.
  - Explanation: Coarse channel values are exactly expectations against bounded single-site features, uniformly in the test.
- **claim-kernel-cutoff-removal** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel-cutoffs)): Let $k=i+j$ be an allowed pair with $j>0$, and let $\lvert g\rvert,\lvert h\rvert\le1$. Replacing the coefficient and remaining-site regularity restrictions in $\mathbb E[\mathcal L_{ik}g(S_i)h(S_k)]$ by no restrictions, while keeping the scalar $k_B$, incurs an absolute error at most $$\label{eq:33} C\frac{\Sigma(j)}{T} \big(r_B+e^{-c_3C_*}\big),\qquad r_B\longrightarrow0.$$ Here $r_B$ may depend on the fixed regularity parameters and on $C_*$, but the constant $C$ is independent of $C_*$, $\eta$, and $C_6$. The pairwise gcd restrictions may then be removed at an additional error $O(B^{-998})$. The resulting integral is $$\label{eq:34} k_B B\sum_{a-b=jc}\gamma_g(b)\gamma_h(a)A_0(c) \rho_0(\log c/B) \Psi_s\big(a/(Tc),b/(Tc)\big).$$ The sum is over positive integers $a,b,c$.
  - Explanation: Regularity restrictions can be removed from the integrated bilinear form with controlled error.
- **claim-kernel-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-kernel-comparison)): For the matrix $\mathcal M$ in <a href="#eq:43" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:43">[eq:43]</a>, put $\mathcal E_{ik}=\mathcal L_{ik}-\mathcal M_{ik}$, with all three matrices zero on nonpairs. Given $\epsilon_1>0$, choose the coarse partition as in <a href="#eq:32" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:32">[eq:32]</a>. Then $$\label{eq:44} \sup_{\substack{g_i,h_k:\Omega_B\to[-1,1]\\ g_i,h_k\ \text{real measurable}}} \frac1M\left\| \sum_{i,k}\mathbb E_{S_i,S_k} [\mathcal E_{ik}(S_i,S_k;s)g_i(S_i)h_k(S_k)]\right\| \le C e^{-c_3C_*}+C_\eta\epsilon_1+o(1).$$ The $o(1)$ is uniform in $s$ and in all the indicated tests. The coefficient of $e^{-c_3C_*}$ is independent of $\eta$, $C_6$, and $C_*$. In addition, for every realization of the site types, $$\label{eq:kernel-main-row-bounds} \sup_i\sum_k\|\mathcal M_{ik}\|\ll_{\eta,m_1}1, \qquad \sup_i\sum_k\|\mathcal M_{ik}\|^2\ll_{\eta,m_1}T^{-1}.$$
  - Explanation: The latent kernel is approximated by a finite-feature matrix under arbitrary bounded single-site tests; the model matrix has bounded rows.
- **claim-sampling-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-sampling-comparison)): For every fixed $\epsilon_2>0$, $$\mathbb E\big\\|\big(\mathcal E_{ik}(S_i,S_k;s)\big)\big\\|_\square \le E_0+\epsilon_2+o(1), \label{eq:45}$$ uniformly for $s$ in the fixed compact range.
  - Explanation: The comparison upgrades to expected cut norm against tests chosen after the matrix is known.
- **claim-sampling-good-event** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sampling-good)): There is a fixed $K>0$ such that, on an event $\mathcal G_B$ of probability $1-O(B^{-0.01})-O(B^{-0.03})$, $$\frac1M\sum_{i,k}\mathcal E_{ik}^2\le B^{-0.20}, \qquad \max_i\sum_k\|\mathcal E_{ik}\|\le K B^{0.07}. \label{eq:46}$$ The event may also be required to satisfy $\max_i\sum_k\mathcal H_{ik}\le K B^{0.07}$. Moreover, the normalized total absolute mass $$A_B=\frac1M\sum_{i,k}\|\mathcal E_{ik}\|$$ has bounded second moment, uniformly in $B$ and $s$.
  - Explanation: A high-probability event controls squared mass and row degrees of the error matrix.
- **claim-sampling-skeleton** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sampling-skeleton)): Put $m_2=\lfloor M B^{-0.18}\rfloor$. On $\mathcal G_B$ there are a subset $J'\subset\{1,\ldots,M\}$ of size $m_2$ and signs $(\delta_j)_{j\in J'}$ such that, with $$\widetilde g_i =\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(\sum_{j\in J'}\mathcal E_{ij}\delta_j\right), \qquad R' =\{1,\ldots,M\}\setminus J',$$ and assigning the sign $+1$ at zero for all sign choices in this section, $$\\|\mathcal E\\|_\square \le\frac1M\sum_{k\in R'} \left\|\sum_{i\in R'}\mathcal E_{ik}\widetilde g_i\right\| +O(B^{-0.01})+O(B^{-0.11}).$$ The constants are independent of the realized matrix on $\mathcal G_B$.
  - Explanation: The cut norm is approximated by signs determined from a small sample of columns.
- **claim-sampling-conditional-mean** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sampling-conditional-mean)): Fix a deterministic subset $J'$ of size $m_2$ and a deterministic assignment $\sigma\in\{-1,1\}^{J'}$. Condition on its site types $\mathbf S_{J'}=\mathbf t$. For $i\in R'$, define the fixed single-site function $$g_i(u)=\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(\sum_{j\in J'} \mathcal E_{ij}(u,t_j;s)\sigma_j\right).$$ Then, writing $$Z_{J',\sigma,\mathbf t} =\frac1M\sum_{k\in R'} \left\|\sum_{i\in R'}\mathcal E_{ik}(S_i,S_k;s)g_i(S_i)\right\|,$$ we have $\mathbb E[Z_{J',\sigma,\mathbf t}\mid\mathbf S_{J'}=\mathbf t] \le E_0+o(1)$, uniformly in $J'$, $\sigma$, $\mathbf t$, and $s$.
  - Explanation: After fixing a column set and signs, the conditional mean of the column test is uniformly controlled.
- **claim-feature-polynomial-approx** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-conclusion-features)): Fix the coarse partition $I=\bigcup_{l=1}^{m_1}I_l$ used in <a href="#eq:43" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:43">[eq:43]</a>. For every $\zeta>0$ there are real polynomials $$p_l(t)=\sum_{v=0}^{d_l}\alpha_{l,v}t^v, \qquad 1\le l\le m_1,$$ independent of $B$, such that the functions $$\widetilde V_{l,B}(S) =\sum_{v=0}^{d_l}\alpha_{l,v} \prod_{p\in S}\frac{1+p^{-v/B}}2$$ satisfy, for all sufficiently large $B$, $$\\|V_l-\widetilde V_{l,B}\\|_{L^2(S)}\le\zeta.$$ Here the norm uses the independent site law with inclusion probabilities $1/p$. For $0<\zeta\le1$, the approximants can be chosen with a common bound depending only on the fixed coarse partition. At actual integer sites they are the fixed finite combinations $$\widetilde V_{l,B}(S_i(n)) =\sum_{v=0}^{d_l}\alpha_{l,v}G_{B,v}(n+i),$$ where $G_{B,v}$ is the function in <a href="#eq:labels-G" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:labels-G">[eq:labels-G]</a> with $P_B=\mathcal P$.
  - Explanation: Coarse features are approximated in L2 by fixed combinations of multiplicative prime products.
- **claim-periodic-lag-approx** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-conclusion-periodic)): For a fixed prime cutoff $P_1\ge2$, define on all integers $j$ $$\mathfrak S_{P_1}(j) =\prod_{\substack{p\le P_1\\p\mid j}}(1-1/p)^{-1} \prod_{\substack{p\le P_1\\p\nmid j}} \left(1-\frac1{(p-1)^2}\right).$$ This is a bounded nonnegative function of period $D=\prod_{p\le P_1}p$. Moreover, $$\lim_{P_1\to\infty}\ \sup_{\substack{T\in\mathbb N\\T\ge1}}\frac1T \sum_{1\le j\le T} \|\mathfrak S(j)-\mathfrak S_{P_1}(j)\|=0,$$ with the supremum taken over positive integers $T$.
  - Explanation: The lag factor is approximated in mean by a periodic function, uniformly in the length.
- **claim-main-energy-vanishes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-conclusion-main-vanishes)): With $C_*$, $\eta$, $C_6$, and the coarse partition fixed as above, $$\lim_{B\to\infty}\ \limsup_{x\to\infty} \|\mathcal Q^{\mathrm{main}}_{B,x}\|=0.$$
  - Explanation: The main feature energy vanishes in the iterated limit, contradicting the positive energy.
- **claim-dickman-marginal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-distribution-dickman)): For every fixed $c\in(0,1)$, $$\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac1x \#\{1\le m\le x:P^+(m)\le x^c\}=\rho(1/c). \label{eq:distribution-dickman}$$ The limit is through all real $x$.
  - Explanation: The density of smooth numbers is the Dickman function of the reciprocal exponent.

```json
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-labels-character-distance",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $J\\geq2$, positive constants $c,C$, a nonprincipal Dirichlet character $\\chi$ of modulus $q_\\chi$, and a finite set $E$ of primes. Suppose that, for each $x$, $h_x$ is a 1-bounded multiplicative function satisfying $$h_x(p)=\\chi(p)\\qquad(p\\leq x^{1/J},\\ p\\notin E).$$ With $X=cx$, one has $$\\label{eq:labels-character-distance}\n \\inf_{|t|\\leq CX}\\mathbb D(h_x,n^{it};X)^2\\longrightarrow\\infty\n \\qquad(x\\to\\infty).$$ The assertion is uniform over all the functions $h_x$ with this prime agreement. The threshold for $x$ may depend on $J,c,C,\\chi,E$.",
        "source_id": "PM-012.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-labels-character-distance",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $J\\geq2$, positive constants $c,C$, a nonprincipal Dirichlet character $\\chi$ of modulus $q_\\chi$, and a finite set $E$ of primes. The threshold for $x$ may depend on $J,c,C,\\chi,E$.",
          "source_id": "PM-012.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-arith-classical-sd",
        "exact_quote": "For each fixed nonnegative integer $H$ and either of the above values of $z$, there are real constants $c_j(z)$ such that, for $Y\\ge3$, $$\\sum_{n\\le Y}g_z(n)\n =Y\\sum_{j=0}^{H}c_j(z)(\\log Y)^{z-1-j}\n       +O_H\\bigl(Y(\\log Y)^{z-2-H}\\bigr),$$ where $$c_0(z)=\\frac1{\\Gamma(z)}\n          \\prod_p(1+z/p)(1-1/p)^z>0.$$ For each fixed $C,D>0$, uniformly over nonprincipal Dirichlet characters $\\chi$ of modulus $d\\le(\\log Y)^C$, one has $$\\sum_{n\\le Y}g_z(n)\\chi(n)\\ll_{C,D}Y(\\log Y)^{-D}.$$ The constants in the second estimate, and the threshold after which it holds, need not be effective.",
        "source_id": "PM-012.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-arith-classical-sd",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed nonnegative integer $H$ and either of the above values of $z$, there are real constants $c_j(z)$ such that, for $Y\\ge3$, $$\\sum_{n\\le Y}g_z(n)\n =Y\\sum_{j=0}^{H}c_j(z)(\\log Y)^{z-1-j}\n       +O_H\\bigl(Y(\\log Y)^{z-2-H}\\bigr),$$ where $$c_0(z)=\\frac1{\\Gamma(z)}\n          \\prod_p(1+z/p)(1-1/p)^z>0.$$ For each fixed $C,D>0$, uniformly over nonprincipal Dirichlet characters $\\chi$ of modulus $d\\le(\\log Y)^C$, one has $$\\sum_{n\\le Y}g_z(n)\\chi(n)\\ll_{C,D}Y(\\log Y)^{-D}.$$ The constants in the second estimate, and the threshold after which it holds, need not be effective.",
          "source_id": "PM-012.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For each fixed nonnegative integer $H$ and either of the above values of $z$, there are real constants $c_j(z)$ such that, for $Y\\ge3$, $$\\sum_{n\\le Y}g_z(n)\n =Y\\sum_{j=0}^{H}c_j(z)(\\log Y)^{z-1-j}\n       +O_H\\bigl(Y(\\log Y)^{z-2-H}\\bigr),$$ where $$c_0(z)=\\frac1{\\Gamma(z)}\n          \\prod_p(1+z/p)(1-1/p)^z>0.$$ For each fixed $C,D>0$, uniformly over nonprincipal Dirichlet characters $\\chi$ of modulus $d\\le(\\log Y)^C$, one has $$\\sum_{n\\le Y}g_z(n)\\chi(n)\\ll_{C,D}Y(\\log Y)^{-D}.$$ The constants in the second estimate, and the threshold after which it holds, need not be effective.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-arith-rough-sd",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $D>0$. There is a fixed integer $H=H(D)$ and real coefficients $C_{j,z}(P_0)$, $0\\le j\\le H$, such that uniformly for $B^{0.89}\\le\\log Y\\le4B$, $$\\sum_{n\\le Y}g_{z,P_0}(n)\n =Y\\sum_{j=0}^{H}C_{j,z}(P_0)(\\log Y)^{z-1-j}\n       +O_D(YB^{-D}).$$ There is an absolute constant $C_0$ such that for every fixed $j$, $$|C_{j,z}(P_0)|\\ll_j(\\log P_0)^{C_0+j},\\qquad\n C_{0,z}(P_0)=c_0(z)\\prod_{p\\le P_0}(1+z/p)^{-1}\n       \\sim\\frac{e^{-\\gamma_{\\rm E}z}}{\\Gamma(z)}(\\log P_0)^{-z},$$ where $\\gamma_{\\rm E}$ is Euler’s constant. Uniformly for nonprincipal $\\chi$ of modulus $d\\le B^{15}$ in the same size range, $$\\sum_{n\\le Y}g_{z,P_0}(n)\\chi(n)\\ll_D YB^{-D}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-012.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-arith-rough-sd",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $D>0$. Uniformly for nonprincipal $\\chi$ of modulus $d\\le B^{15}$ in the same size range, $$\\sum_{n\\le Y}g_{z,P_0}(n)\\chi(n)\\ll_D YB^{-D}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-012.1"

      "id": "claim-rough-selberg-delange",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-arith-local-laws",
        "exact_quote": "Let $w_3$ be smooth and supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$. Uniformly for $0.9B\\le\\log X\\le2.2B$, $q\\le B^{15}$, $(u,q)=1$, and real $\\xi$ with $|\\xi|\\le B^{14}$, $$\\sum_c A_0(c)w_3(c/X)e(cu/q+\\xi c/X)\n =X\\frac{\\mu_{\\rm Mob}(q)}{\\varphi(q)}\n       \\int w_3(t)m_B(\\log(Xt)/B)e(\\xi t)\\,dt\n       +O(XB^{-50}).\n \\label{eq:1}$$ The convention $q=1$ is included. The constant in the error is controlled by a fixed constant times finitely many low-order smooth norms of $w_3$; the expansion order defining $m_B$ is independent of $w_3$.*\n\n\n\n\n*Let $\\nu_z$ be the law of the product of primes of $\\mathcal P$ selected independently with probabilities $z/p$, and write $s=\\log a/B$ for its log coordinate. For $d\\le B^{15}$, every such product is a unit modulo $d$. Uniformly for all intervals $I'\\subset[B^{-1/10},3.2]$ and unit residues $r\\pmod d$, $$\\mathbb P_{\\nu_z}(s\\in I',\\ a\\equiv r\\pmod d)\n =\\frac1{\\varphi(d)}\\int_{I'}f_{z,B}(s)\\,ds+O(B^{-80}).\n \\label{eq:2}$$ Uniformly for $0\\le\\delta\\le1$, $$\\mathbb P_{\\nu_z}(s\\le\\delta)\\ll(\\delta+1/R)^z.\n \\label{eq:3}$$ For each positive integer $n\\le\\exp(3.2B)$, $$\\nu_{1/2}(n)\\ll\\frac{A_0(n)}{Bn},\n \\label{eq:4}$$ where both sides are zero unless $n$ is a squarefree rough product, apart from the allowed empty product $n=1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-012.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-arith-local-laws",
          "exact_quote": "Let $w_3$ be smooth and supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$. The constant in the error is controlled by a fixed constant times finitely many low-order smooth norms of $w_3$; the expansion order defining $m_B$ is independent of $w_3$.*\n\n\n\n\n*Let $\\nu_z$ be the law of the product of primes of $\\mathcal P$ selected independently with probabilities $z/p$, and write $s=\\log a/B$ for its log coordinate. For $d\\le B^{15}$, every such product is a unit modulo $d$. Uniformly for all intervals $I'\\subset[B^{-1/10},3.2]$ and unit residues $r\\pmod d$, $$\\mathbb P_{\\nu_z}(s\\in I',\\ a\\equiv r\\pmod d)\n =\\frac1{\\varphi(d)}\\int_{I'}f_{z,B}(s)\\,ds+O(B^{-80}).\n \\label{eq:2}$$ Uniformly for $0\\le\\delta\\le1$, $$\\mathbb P_{\\nu_z}(s\\le\\delta)\\ll(\\delta+1/R)^z.\n \\label{eq:3}$$ For each positive integer $n\\le\\exp(3.2B)$, $$\\nu_{1/2}(n)\\ll\\frac{A_0(n)}{Bn},\n \\label{eq:4}$$ where both sides are zero unless $n$ is a squarefree rough product, apart from the allowed empty product $n=1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-012.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $w_3$ be smooth and supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$. The constant in the error is controlled by a fixed constant times finitely many low-order smooth norms of $w_3$; the expansion order defining $m_B$ is independent of $w_3$.*\n\n\n\n\n*Let $\\nu_z$ be the law of the product of primes of $\\mathcal P$ selected independently with probabilities $z/p$, and write $s=\\log a/B$ for its log coordinate. For $d\\le B^{15}$, every such product is a unit modulo $d$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-arith-weighted-sieve",
        "exact_quote": "There is a sufficiently small $c_k>0$, depending only on fixed $k$, with the following properties for $Z\\ge2$.*\n\n*In an interval of length $N\\ge1$, at most $k$ forbidden residues at each prime $p\\le Z$ give an upper bound $$C_kN\\prod_{p\\le Z}(1-\\theta_p)+O_k(N^{0.8})\n       \\qquad(Z\\le N^{c_k}).$$ In a rectangle of side lengths $N_1,N_2\\ge1$, suppose that at each prime the forbidden set is a union of at most $k$ proper affine lines. With $N_* =\\min(N_1,N_2)$, there is the corresponding bound $$C_kN_1N_2\\prod_{p\\le Z}(1-\\theta_p)\n       +O_k(N_1N_2N_*^{-0.4})\n       \\qquad(Z\\le N_*^{c_k}).$$ An application may omit specified primes from the restrictions, provided it also omits their factors from the products.*\n\n*Both assertions also apply to reducing local weights. At each prime, attach a factor in $[0,1]$ to each of at most $k$ specified residue classes modulo $p$ in the interval case, or at most $k$ proper affine lines in the rectangle case. The factor is applied on its class or line and equals one off it. Replace $1-\\theta_p$ by the residue average of the product of these factors.",
        "source_id": "PM-012.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-arith-weighted-sieve",
          "exact_quote": "There is a sufficiently small $c_k>0$, depending only on fixed $k$, with the following properties for $Z\\ge2$.*\n\n*In an interval of length $N\\ge1$, at most $k$ forbidden residues at each prime $p\\le Z$ give an upper bound $$C_kN\\prod_{p\\le Z}(1-\\theta_p)+O_k(N^{0.8})\n       \\qquad(Z\\le N^{c_k}).$$ In a rectangle of side lengths $N_1,N_2\\ge1$, suppose that at each prime the forbidden set is a union of at most $k$ proper affine lines. With $N_* =\\min(N_1,N_2)$, there is the corresponding bound $$C_kN_1N_2\\prod_{p\\le Z}(1-\\theta_p)\n       +O_k(N_1N_2N_*^{-0.4})\n       \\qquad(Z\\le N_*^{c_k}).$$ An application may omit specified primes from the restrictions, provided it also omits their factors from the products.*\n\n*Both assertions also apply to reducing local weights. At each prime, attach a factor in $[0,1]$ to each of at most $k$ specified residue classes modulo $p$ in the interval case, or at most $k$ proper affine lines in the rectangle case. The factor is applied on its class or line and equals one off it. Replace $1-\\theta_p$ by the residue average of the product of these factors.",
          "source_id": "PM-012.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-arith-coefficient-moments",
        "exact_quote": "On these intervals, $$X^{-1}\\sum_{n\\asymp X}A_0(n)\\ll1,\\qquad\n X^{-1}\\sum_{n\\asymp X}A_0(n)^2\\ll B^{1/4+o(1)}.\n \\label{eq:5}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-012.1"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-arith-coefficient-moments",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-arith-form-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "Uniformly for $0<|j|\\le T$ and squarefree rough $b\\asymp TX$, $$\\sum_{\\substack{c\\asymp X\\\\b+jc\\asymp TX,\\ b+jc>0}}\n               A_0(c)A_0(b+jc)\\ll X\\Sigma(j).\n \\label{eq:6}$$ On the same fixed log-size ranges, $$\\sum_{\\substack{b\\asymp TX,\\ c\\asymp X\\\\a=b+jc\\asymp TX,\\ a>0}}\n               A_0(b)A_0(a)A_0(c)\\ll TX^2\\Sigma(j).\n \\label{eq:7}$$ The implied constants do not depend on $b,j,X$, or $B$.",
        "source_id": "PM-012.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-arith-regularity-loss",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute $c_3>0$ such that, for each fixed choice of the grid, tolerance, and compact log-size ranges above, there are $C>0$ and a function $\\epsilon_B\\to0$ such that the part of the untruncated sum in <a href=\"#eq:7\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:7\">[eq:7]</a> where at least one coefficient is not regular is at most $$C TX^2\\Sigma(j)\\bigl(\\epsilon_B+e^{-c_3C_*}\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:9}$$ The constant $C$ may depend on the fixed grid, tolerance, and scale ranges, but not on $C_*,j,X,B$. The function $\\epsilon_B$ may depend on the same fixed data but is independent of $C_*$. The analogous bound with scale $X$ applies to the first-moment sum of $A_0$ in <a href=\"#eq:5\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:5\">[eq:5]</a>. For the product of two independent coefficient weights in a box, it applies with the corresponding area in place of $TX^2\\Sigma(j)$.*\n\n*The same upper probability $O(\\epsilon_B+e^{-c_3C_*})$ holds for failure of regularity in a set of independent prime indicators on $\\mathcal P$ with parameters $1/(2p)+O(p^{-2})$, uniformly when the constant in the $O$ term is fixed. It also holds after omitting a deterministic subset $E_B\\subset\\mathcal P$ satisfying $$\\sum_{p\\in E_B}\\frac1p\\le C_E\\frac{B}{P_0}$$ for a fixed $C_E>0$. The subset may depend on $B$, and the bound is uniform over all such subsets for each fixed $C_E$.",
        "source_id": "PM-012.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-amp-small-ball",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $H>0$. Write $L=\\log P_0$. For sufficiently large $B$, let $L\\le Y\\le8B$, put $U=\\min(Y,4B)$, and form a random product $Q_Y$ by including each prime $P_0<p\\le e^U$ independently with probability $1/(4p)$. Uniformly over all intervals $J\\subset\\mathbb R$ of length at most $H$, $$\\mathbb P\\bigl(\\log Q_Y\\in J\\cap[Y/2,\\infty)\\bigr)\n \\ll_H\\frac1Y.$$ The implicit constant is independent of $B$, $Y$, and $J$.",
        "source_id": "PM-012.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-amp-small-ball",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $H>0$. Write $L=\\log P_0$. Uniformly over all intervals $J\\subset\\mathbb R$ of length at most $H$, $$\\mathbb P\\bigl(\\log Q_Y\\in J\\cap[Y/2,\\infty)\\bigr)\n \\ll_H\\frac1Y.$$ The implicit constant is independent of $B$, $Y$, and $J$.",
          "source_id": "PM-012.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix $H>0$. Write $L=\\log P_0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-amp-energy",
        "exact_quote": "There is $c_5>0$, independent of all sufficiently large fixed $C_*$, for which the nonnegative energy below satisfies the displayed lower bound: $$\\label{eq:15}\n\\begin{gathered}\n I_{2,x}\n = \\frac1x\\sum_{c\\ge1}\n   \\rho_0(\\log c/B)A(c)\n   \\sum_{m\\ge1}K(m)\n   \\left|\n     \\sum_{\\substack{a\\ge1\\\\c\\mid am+1}}\n     A(a)K(l_a)\\psi(a/(Tc))\n     \\phi(am/x)F_x(am+1)\n   \\right|^2,\n \\\\\n \\liminf_{B\\to\\infty}\\ \\liminf_{x\\to\\infty}\n       \\frac{I_{2,x}}{BT}\\ge c_5\n\\end{gathered}$$ Its diagonal contribution is negligible: $$\\lim_{B\\to\\infty}\\ \\limsup_{x\\to\\infty}\n       \\frac{I_{2,x}^{\\mathrm{diag}}}{BT}=0.$$ Here $I_{2,x}^{\\mathrm{diag}}$ denotes the terms with equal coefficient indices on expanding the square, and $l_a=(am+1)/c$ as in <a href=\"#eq:10\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:10\">[eq:10]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-012.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-amp-energy",
          "exact_quote": "There is $c_5>0$, independent of all sufficiently large fixed $C_*$, for which the nonnegative energy below satisfies the displayed lower bound: $$\\label{eq:15}\n\\begin{gathered}\n I_{2,x}\n = \\frac1x\\sum_{c\\ge1}\n   \\rho_0(\\log c/B)A(c)\n   \\sum_{m\\ge1}K(m)\n   \\left|\n     \\sum_{\\substack{a\\ge1\\\\c\\mid am+1}}\n     A(a)K(l_a)\\psi(a/(Tc))\n     \\phi(am/x)F_x(am+1)\n   \\right|^2,\n \\\\\n \\liminf_{B\\to\\infty}\\ \\liminf_{x\\to\\infty}\n       \\frac{I_{2,x}}{BT}\\ge c_5\n\\end{gathered}$$ Its diagonal contribution is negligible: $$\\lim_{B\\to\\infty}\\ \\limsup_{x\\to\\infty}\n       \\frac{I_{2,x}^{\\mathrm{diag}}}{BT}=0.$$ Here $I_{2,x}^{\\mathrm{diag}}$ denotes the terms with equal coefficient indices on expanding the square, and $l_a=(am+1)/c$ as in <a href=\"#eq:10\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:10\">[eq:10]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-012.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is $c_5>0$, independent of all sufficiently large fixed $C_*$, for which the nonnegative energy below satisfies the displayed lower bound: $$\\label{eq:15}\n\\begin{gathered}\n I_{2,x}\n = \\frac1x\\sum_{c\\ge1}\n   \\rho_0(\\log c/B)A(c)\n   \\sum_{m\\ge1}K(m)\n   \\left|\n     \\sum_{\\substack{a\\ge1\\\\c\\mid am+1}}\n     A(a)K(l_a)\\psi(a/(Tc))\n     \\phi(am/x)F_x(am+1)\n   \\right|^2,\n \\\\\n \\liminf_{B\\to\\infty}\\ \\liminf_{x\\to\\infty}\n       \\frac{I_{2,x}}{BT}\\ge c_5\n\\end{gathered}$$ Its diagonal contribution is negligible: $$\\lim_{B\\to\\infty}\\ \\limsup_{x\\to\\infty}\n       \\frac{I_{2,x}^{\\mathrm{diag}}}{BT}=0.$$ Here $I_{2,x}^{\\mathrm{diag}}$ denotes the terms with equal coefficient indices on expanding the square, and $l_a=(am+1)/c$ as in <a href=\"#eq:10\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:10\">[eq:10]</a>.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-edge-mass",
        "exact_quote": "Uniformly for $0<|j|\\le T$ and $s\\in\\mathcal S$, $$\\label{eq:18}\n \\mathbb E_{u\\in\\widehat{\\mathbb Z}}\\mathcal K_j(u,s)\\ll\\frac{\\Sigma(j)}T.$$ The implied constant can be independent of the regularity parameter $C_*$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-graph-root-comparison",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-moments-rows",
        "exact_quote": "For a sufficiently fine fixed regularity grid and then a sufficiently small fixed tolerance, the independent-site latent kernel satisfies $$\\sum_{k\\ne i}\\mathbb E\\,\\mathcal L_{ik}^{2}\\ll B^{-.21}\n \\qquad (1\\le i\\le M).\n \\label{eq:24}$$ Moreover, its normalized total mass has bounded second moment: $$\\mathbb E\\left(\\frac1M\\sum_{i,k}\\mathcal L_{ik}\\right)^2\\ll1.$$ For every set $I\\subseteq\\{1,\\ldots,M\\}$, the same estimates hold after replacing $\\mathcal L_{ik}$ by $\\mathbf 1_{\\{i,k\\in I\\}}\\mathcal L_{ik}$; the normalization remains $M$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-channels-features",
        "exact_quote": "For the chosen coarse partition, define $$V_l(S)=\\frac1{|I_l|}\n          \\mathbb P_{\\rm split}(x_b\\in I_l\\mid S).$$ Then $0\\le V_l\\le |I_l|^{-1}$, and for every $g\\in L^2(\\Omega_B)$ the coarse value of $P_{m_1}u_g$ on $I_l$ is exactly $$\\mathbb E[g(S)V_l(S)].$$ In particular the bounds <a href=\"#eq:31\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:31\">[eq:31]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:32\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:32\">[eq:32]</a> and this identity hold uniformly when $g$ varies with a position, with $B$, or with an external parameter.",
        "source_id": "PM-012.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-channels-features",
          "exact_quote": "For the chosen coarse partition, define $$V_l(S)=\\frac1{|I_l|}\n          \\mathbb P_{\\rm split}(x_b\\in I_l\\mid S).$$ Then $0\\le V_l\\le |I_l|^{-1}$, and for every $g\\in L^2(\\Omega_B)$ the coarse value of $P_{m_1}u_g$ on $I_l$ is exactly $$\\mathbb E[g(S)V_l(S)].$$ In particular the bounds <a href=\"#eq:31\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:31\">[eq:31]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:32\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:32\">[eq:32]</a> and this identity hold uniformly when $g$ varies with a position, with $B$, or with an external parameter.",
          "source_id": "PM-012.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the chosen coarse partition, define $$V_l(S)=\\frac1{|I_l|}\n          \\mathbb P_{\\rm split}(x_b\\in I_l\\mid S).$$ Then $0\\le V_l\\le |I_l|^{-1}$, and for every $g\\in L^2(\\Omega_B)$ the coarse value of $P_{m_1}u_g$ on $I_l$ is exactly $$\\mathbb E[g(S)V_l(S)].$$ In particular the bounds <a href=\"#eq:31\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:31\">[eq:31]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:32\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:32\">[eq:32]</a> and this identity hold uniformly when $g$ varies with a position, with $B$, or with an external parameter.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-kernel-cutoffs",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k=i+j$ be an allowed pair with $j>0$, and let $\\lvert g\\rvert,\\lvert h\\rvert\\le1$. Replacing the coefficient and remaining-site regularity restrictions in $\\mathbb E[\\mathcal L_{ik}g(S_i)h(S_k)]$ by no restrictions, while keeping the scalar $k_B$, incurs an absolute error at most $$\\label{eq:33}\n C\\frac{\\Sigma(j)}{T}\n       \\big(r_B+e^{-c_3C_*}\\big),\\qquad r_B\\longrightarrow0.$$ Here $r_B$ may depend on the fixed regularity parameters and on $C_*$, but the constant $C$ is independent of $C_*$, $\\eta$, and $C_6$. The pairwise gcd restrictions may then be removed at an additional error $O(B^{-998})$. The resulting integral is $$\\label{eq:34}\n k_B B\\sum_{a-b=jc}\\gamma_g(b)\\gamma_h(a)A_0(c)\n       \\rho_0(\\log c/B)\n       \\Psi_s\\big(a/(Tc),b/(Tc)\\big).$$ The sum is over positive integers $a,b,c$.",
        "source_id": "PM-012.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-kernel-cutoffs",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k=i+j$ be an allowed pair with $j>0$, and let $\\lvert g\\rvert,\\lvert h\\rvert\\le1$. Replacing the coefficient and remaining-site regularity restrictions in $\\mathbb E[\\mathcal L_{ik}g(S_i)h(S_k)]$ by no restrictions, while keeping the scalar $k_B$, incurs an absolute error at most $$\\label{eq:33}\n C\\frac{\\Sigma(j)}{T}\n       \\big(r_B+e^{-c_3C_*}\\big),\\qquad r_B\\longrightarrow0.$$ Here $r_B$ may depend on the fixed regularity parameters and on $C_*$, but the constant $C$ is independent of $C_*$, $\\eta$, and $C_6$. The pairwise gcd restrictions may then be removed at an additional error $O(B^{-998})$. The resulting integral is $$\\label{eq:34}\n k_B B\\sum_{a-b=jc}\\gamma_g(b)\\gamma_h(a)A_0(c)\n       \\rho_0(\\log c/B)\n       \\Psi_s\\big(a/(Tc),b/(Tc)\\big).$$ The sum is over positive integers $a,b,c$.",
          "source_id": "PM-012.1"
        }

Given $\\epsilon_1>0$, choose the coarse partition as in <a href=\"#eq:32\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:32\">[eq:32]</a>.         "exact_quote": "Put $m_2=\\lfloor M B^{-0.18}\\rfloor$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sampling-conditional-mean",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a deterministic subset $J'$ of size $m_2$ and a deterministic assignment $\\sigma\\in\\{-1,1\\}^{J'}$. Condition on its site types $\\mathbf S_{J'}=\\mathbf t$. For $i\\in R'$, define the fixed single-site function $$g_i(u)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{sgn}}\\left(\\sum_{j\\in J'}\n                 \\mathcal E_{ij}(u,t_j;s)\\sigma_j\\right).$$ Then, writing $$Z_{J',\\sigma,\\mathbf t}\n   =\\frac1M\\sum_{k\\in R'}\n       \\left|\\sum_{i\\in R'}\\mathcal E_{ik}(S_i,S_k;s)g_i(S_i)\\right|,$$ we have $\\mathbb E[Z_{J',\\sigma,\\mathbf t}\\mid\\mathbf S_{J'}=\\mathbf t]\n\\le E_0+o(1)$, uniformly in $J'$, $\\sigma$, $\\mathbf t$, and $s$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-conclusion-features",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the coarse partition $I=\\bigcup_{l=1}^{m_1}I_l$ used in <a href=\"#eq:43\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:43\">[eq:43]</a>. For every $\\zeta>0$ there are real polynomials $$p_l(t)=\\sum_{v=0}^{d_l}\\alpha_{l,v}t^v,\n \\qquad 1\\le l\\le m_1,$$ independent of $B$, such that the functions $$\\widetilde V_{l,B}(S)\n   =\\sum_{v=0}^{d_l}\\alpha_{l,v}\n       \\prod_{p\\in S}\\frac{1+p^{-v/B}}2$$ satisfy, for all sufficiently large $B$, $$\\|V_l-\\widetilde V_{l,B}\\|_{L^2(S)}\\le\\zeta.$$ Here the norm uses the independent site law with inclusion probabilities $1/p$. For $0<\\zeta\\le1$, the approximants can be chosen with a common bound depending only on the fixed coarse partition. At actual integer sites they are the fixed finite combinations $$\\widetilde V_{l,B}(S_i(n))\n       =\\sum_{v=0}^{d_l}\\alpha_{l,v}G_{B,v}(n+i),$$ where $G_{B,v}$ is the function in <a href=\"#eq:labels-G\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:labels-G\">[eq:labels-G]</a> with $P_B=\\mathcal P$.",

For $0<\\zeta\\le1$, the approximants can be chosen with a common bound depending only on the fixed coarse partition.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-conclusion-periodic",
        "exact_quote": "For a fixed prime cutoff $P_1\\ge2$, define on all integers $j$ $$\\mathfrak S_{P_1}(j)\n =\\prod_{\\substack{p\\le P_1\\\\p\\mid j}}(1-1/p)^{-1}\n   \\prod_{\\substack{p\\le P_1\\\\p\\nmid j}}\n       \\left(1-\\frac1{(p-1)^2}\\right).$$ This is a bounded nonnegative function of period $D=\\prod_{p\\le P_1}p$. Moreover, $$\\lim_{P_1\\to\\infty}\\ \n   \\sup_{\\substack{T\\in\\mathbb N\\\\T\\ge1}}\\frac1T\n       \\sum_{1\\le j\\le T}\n         |\\mathfrak S(j)-\\mathfrak S_{P_1}(j)|=0,$$ with the supremum taken over positive integers $T$.",
        "source_id": "PM-012.1"
      },
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Dickman-de Bruijn function** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The Dickman–de Bruijn function $\rho$ is the continuous function on $[0,\infty)$ determined by $$\rho(u)=1\quad(0\leq u\leq1),\qquad u\rho'(u)=-\rho(u-1)\quad(u>1).$$
- **Regular prime set** ([source](#stmt-def-arith-regularity)): For $S\subset\mathcal P$ put $$N_g(S)=\begin{cases} \|\{p\in S:\log p\le B^g\}\|,&g<1,\\ \|S\|,&g=1. \end{cases}$$ Let $Y_i=2^i\log P_0$, $0\le i\le i_{\max}$, where $i_{\max}$ is the first index with $Y_i\ge4B$. The set $S$ is regular if both of the following conditions hold: $$(g/2-\tau)\ell\le N_g(S)\le(g/2+\tau)\ell \quad\hbox{for every grid point }g,$$ and $$\|\{p\in S:\log p>Y_i\}\| \ge0.4\log(B/Y_i)-C_* \quad(0\le i\le i_{\max}).$$ An integer or profinite integer is regular when its set of dividing primes from $\mathcal P$ is regular. Multiplicities are not counted in these cutoffs.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-labels** [claim](#stmt-lem-labels-marginal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-labels-marginal): Factorial moments of bin counts via prime estimates and progression counting. -> label means and multiplier invariance
- **step-short-averages** [claim](#stmt-lem-labels-short) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-labels-character-distance-2): Interpolate by multiplicative functions and apply real and complex short-interval theorems. -> vanishing weighted short averages
- **step-arith** [claim](#stmt-prop-arith-local-laws) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-arith-local-laws): Selberg-Delange expansions and rough-cutoff convolution. -> local densities for coefficients
- **step-amplification** [claim](#stmt-prop-amp-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-amp-energy): Divisor amplifier, Cauchy-Schwarz and positive energy. -> positive off-diagonal energy
- **step-graph** [claim](#stmt-prop-graph-root-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-graph-root-comparison): Change of variables and coupling to independent sites. -> latent kernel in cut norm
- **step-kernel** [claim](#stmt-prop-kernel-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-kernel-comparison): Fourier detection, channel smoothing and coarse features. -> finite-feature matrix
- **step-sampling** [claim](#stmt-prop-sampling-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sampling-conditional-mean-2): Column sampling and bounded-differences concentration. -> adaptive cut norm bound
- **step-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-prop-conclusion-main-vanishes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-conclusion-main-vanishes): Feature and periodic approximation, block subdivision, short averages. -> main energy vanishes, contradiction
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-distribution-dickman-2): Fourier inversion, Dickman marginal, threshold sandwich. -> joint Dickman law

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-joint-dickman-product**: For every fixed pair of exponents in (0,1), the natural density of integers n whose largest prime factors of n and n+1 are bounded by the stated powers of n equals the product of the two one-variable Dickman values; the limit is over all real X with ordinary unweighted counting.
- **inv-ordering-half**: The set of n with P^+(n) < P^+(n+1) has natural density one half, and the reverse ordering has the same density.
- **inv-mixed-decorrelation**: For every fixed number of bins and every pair of fixed unit-modulus phase vectors, the mixed average of the conjugated second label against the centered first label at consecutive integers tends to zero along integer scales.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-shrinking-exponents**: Apply the joint law to smoothness bounds whose exponents shrink with the scale, as in subexponential smoothness bases. -> The theorem gives no density statement when the exponents depend on X; independence is not asserted there.
- **anti-weighted-sampling**: Use the product law for integers drawn with non-uniform or logarithmic weights. -> The statement concerns ordinary natural density only; other weightings are not covered.
- **anti-quantitative-rate**: Quote an error term or uniformity for growing parameters from this theorem. -> The result is a pure limit; no rate of convergence is supplied, so finite-range yields cannot be bounded from it.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are asymptotic limit statements in natural density, proved by contradiction through compactness, weak limits of measures on the profinite integers and sampling arguments with no explicit constants or finite procedure; there is no finite constructive object or algorithm that a runnable reference implementation and oracle could decide.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an asymptotic density statement about largest prime factors of consecutive integers. It states no routine, interface or data flow that a program could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns simulation dynamics, procedural generation or sampling schemes. The only probabilistic content is a limiting law of natural density. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool, checker or workflow is defined. The proof is an analytic contradiction argument, not a procedure that a tool could run. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The argument is by contradiction along a subsequence of scales and extracts a profile on the profinite integers. It gives no finite procedure, so no pseudocode or complexity is available. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The labels, bins and divisor weights are analytic objects used inside estimates. The paper does not propose a storage or indexing layout. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no arithmetic circuit, parallel scheme or throughput model. Its limits are taken as the scale tends to infinity, which gives no sizing guidance for a device. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem needs fixed exponents and an asymptotic limit. Practical factoring and discrete-logarithm sieves use smoothness bounds that shrink with the input, which this result does not cover. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A sampling step appears, but it is a proof device for approximating cut norms of arithmetic kernels. The paper has no learning problem, model or training objective. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or solver is considered. Cut norms are used only as an error metric inside the proof, not as something to optimise. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no protocol, partitioning or consensus setting. The only balance statement is a density one half for an ordering of two largest prime factors. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result belongs to analytic number theory and is a theoretical statement about natural density. Any engineering use would need new constructive and effective content that the paper does not supply. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-exponents** (hypothesis): The joint law is proved only for exponents that are fixed constants in the open unit interval; nothing is asserted when the exponents change with the scale. Blocked use: ['Applying the product formula to smoothness bounds whose exponents shrink as the integer size grows', 'Reading the limit as a statement about subexponential smoothness bases']
- **limit-ordinary-counting** (hypothesis): The limit is over all real X with ordinary unweighted counting; logarithmic or otherwise weighted densities are different statements. Blocked use: ['Using the product law for integers drawn with non-uniform or weighted sampling', 'Substituting a logarithmic-density average for the ordinary natural density']
- **limit-no-quantitative-rate** (formal_scope): The theorem is a pure limit at fixed parameters. It does not assert a quantitative error term or uniformity for growing parameters, so no finite-range bound follows from it. Blocked use: ['Extracting an explicit error bound for a finite range of n', 'Claiming uniformity of the joint law in growing smoothness parameters']
- **limit-contradiction-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The decorrelation result is obtained by assuming it fails along a sequence of scales and deriving a contradiction from a limiting profile; the argument supplies no finite procedure and no explicit threshold. Blocked use: ['Reading the proof as an algorithm that computes or certifies the joint density', 'Extracting an explicit scale beyond which the density is close to the limit']
- **limit-fixed-multiplier** (hypothesis): Invariance of the centred and unit-modulus labels under a multiplier holds only for a fixed multiplier and only once the scale is large enough in terms of that multiplier and the number of bins. Blocked use: ['Applying the invariance to multipliers that grow together with the scale', 'Using the invariance at scales that are not large relative to the multiplier']
- **limit-iterated-limit-order** (approximation): The short-average estimates are stated for the iterated limit in which the counting scale tends to infinity before the auxiliary parameter; they are not joint-limit statements. Blocked use: ['Letting the counting scale and the auxiliary parameter grow simultaneously in these estimates', 'Interchanging the order of the two limits']
- **limit-ineffective-constants** (nonconstructive): The twisted Selberg-Delange estimate holds with constants and a threshold that need not be effective, so the arithmetic input cannot be turned into explicit numerical bounds. Blocked use: ['Computing an explicit threshold for the character-twisted sum', 'Treating the arithmetic preliminaries as an effective numerical result']
- **limit-local-law-ranges** (hypothesis): The local coefficient law is stated only for moduli and frequencies bounded by fixed powers of the auxiliary parameter and for logarithmic sizes in a fixed window. Blocked use: ['Applying the local law with moduli or frequencies beyond the stated powers of the parameter', 'Applying the local law outside the stated logarithmic size window']
- **limit-unit-shift** (edge_case): The amplifier and the change of variables are built for the factorizations of n and n+1; the argument is not stated for other shifts or for polynomial values. Blocked use: ['Extending the joint law to pairs n and n plus a larger fixed shift without a new argument', 'Applying the theorem to values of polynomials of higher degree']

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 012 (`lean/docs/012.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The joint Dickman law for consecutive integers](../../preprints/The-joint-Dickman-law-for-consecutive-integers-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Let $P^+(n)$ be the largest prime factor of $n$. The formalization proves the joint Dickman law in ordinary natural density: for every $0<a,b<1$, the density of integers satisfying $P^+(n)\le n^a$ and $P^+(n+1)\le n^b$ tends to $\rho(1/a)\rho(1/b)$, where $\rho$ is the Dickman function.
>
> It also proves that each of the orderings $P^+(n)<P^+(n+1)$ and $P^+(n+1)<P^+(n)$ has natural density $1/2$.

Comparator source `JointDickman.lean` (2093 bytes, sha256 `b45c341c7339e3cf13b6c62ffea0c815739b5b3a27043bfa0fca05f8b3909141`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! # The joint Dickman law for consecutive integers -/

namespace Erdos970.NumberTheoryLean.DelayConstruction

noncomputable def kernel (f : ℝ → ℝ) (t : ℝ) : ℝ := f (t - 1) / max 1 t

noncomputable def stepApprox (σ : ℝ) : ℕ → ℝ → ℝ
  | 0 => fun _ => 1
  | n + 1 => fun u => 1 + σ * ∫ t in (1 : ℝ)..max 1 u, kernel (stepApprox σ n) t

noncomputable def delay (σ u : ℝ) : ℝ := stepApprox σ ⌈u⌉₊ u

end Erdos970.NumberTheoryLean.DelayConstruction

namespace Erdos970.NumberTheoryLean.Dickman

/-- The continuous delay-equation construction of the Dickman function. -/
noncomputable def rho : ℝ → ℝ := DelayConstruction.delay (-1)

end Erdos970.NumberTheoryLean.Dickman

namespace JointDickman

noncomputable def empiricalCount (P : ℕ → Prop) (N : ℕ) : ℕ := by
  classical
  exact ((Finset.Icc 2 N).filter P).card

/-- Ordinary natural density over `2 ≤ n ≤ X`, normalized by the real endpoint. -/
noncomputable def realDensity (P : ℕ → Prop) (X : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (empiricalCount P ⌊X⌋₊ : ℝ) / X

end JointDickman

namespace JointDickmanPaper

open Filter JointDickman
open scoped Topology

/-- Theorem 1: ordinary natural density, with both thresholds based at `n`. -/
theorem joint_law (a b : ℝ) (ha : 0 < a) (ha1 : a < 1) (hb : 0 < b) (hb1 : b < 1) :
    Tendsto (realDensity (fun n =>
      (n.maxPrimeFac : ℝ) ≤ (n : ℝ) ^ a ∧ ((n + 1).maxPrimeFac : ℝ) ≤ (n : ℝ) ^ b))
      atTop (𝓝 (Erdos970.NumberTheoryLean.Dickman.rho (1 / a) *
        Erdos970.NumberTheoryLean.Dickman.rho (1 / b))) := by
  sorry

/-- Corollary 2: the increasing strict ordering has natural density one half. -/
theorem increasing_order :
    Tendsto (realDensity (fun n => n.maxPrimeFac < (n + 1).maxPrimeFac))
      atTop (𝓝 (1 / 2)) := by
  sorry

/-- The reverse strict ordering also has natural density one half. -/
theorem decreasing_order :
    Tendsto (realDensity (fun n => (n + 1).maxPrimeFac < n.maxPrimeFac))
      atTop (𝓝 (1 / 2)) := by
  sorry

end JointDickmanPaper

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let $`P^+(n)`$ denote the largest prime factor of <i>n</i>. We prove that $`\log P^+(n)/\log n`$ and $`\log P^+(n+1)/\log n`$ are asymptotically independent in ordinary natural density, with Dickman marginals. This resolves the Erdős–Pomerance joint Dickman conjecture positively and implies that the ordering $`P^+(n)\lt P^+(n+1)`$ has natural density 1/2.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For an integer $n\geq2$, let $P^+(n)$ denote its largest prime divisor, and put $P^+(1)=1$. The Dickman–de Bruijn function $\rho$ is the continuous function on $[0,\infty)$ determined by $$\rho(u)=1\quad(0\leq u\leq1),\qquad
 u\rho'(u)=-\rho(u-1)\quad(u>1).$$ The classical theory of smooth numbers, beginning with Dickman and developed by Ramaswami and de Bruijn (Dickman 1930; Ramaswami 1949; de Bruijn 1951) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Dickman1930](#ref-Dickman1930), [Ramaswami1949](#ref-Ramaswami1949), [deBruijn1951](#ref-deBruijn1951)], gives $$\frac1X\#\{n\leq X:P^+(n)\leq X^a\}
 \longrightarrow \rho(1/a)\qquad(0<a\leq1).$$ Our result identifies the joint law at two consecutive integers.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Joint Dickman law). *For every fixed $a,b\in(0,1)$, $$\lim_{X\to\infty}\frac1X
 \#\{2\leq n\leq X:P^+(n)\leq n^a,
                         \ P^+(n+1)\leq n^b\}
 =\rho(1/a)\rho(1/b).$$ Here the limit is taken over all real $X$, with ordinary, unweighted counting.*

</div>

Thus $\log P^+(n)/\log n$ and $\log P^+(n+1)/\log n$ have, in natural density, independent limiting distributions with distribution function $a\mapsto\rho(1/a)$, continuously extended to $[0,1]$. Section <a href="#sec:distribution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:distribution">11</a> proves the corresponding law with fixed thresholds $X^a,X^b$, derives the displayed moving-threshold statement, and identifies this continuous limiting law. Inclusion–exclusion using the fixed-threshold law and its marginals gives the upper-tail independence conjecture of Erdős and Pomerance (Erdős and Pomerance 1978, 311) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[ErdosPomerance1978](#ref-ErdosPomerance1978)] <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[ErdosPomerance1978](#ref-ErdosPomerance1978)].

The comparison conjecture usually attributed to Erdős and Turán is a consequence, rather than a separate main theorem.

<a id="stmt-cor-comparison"></a>
<div id="cor:comparison" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *One has $$\lim_{X\to\infty}\frac1X
 \#\{2\leq n\leq X:P^+(n)<P^+(n+1)\}=\frac12.$$ The reverse ordering has the same natural density.*

</div>

The limiting marginal is continuous, so its product measure gives zero mass to the diagonal; symmetry then gives the corollary. In particular, no quantitative separation estimate is needed for this deduction.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Previous work

Erdős and Pomerance (Erdős and Pomerance 1978) formulated the joint independence problem and proved that each ordering has positive lower natural density. Their explicit lower bound was $0.0099$. They also proved that for every $\varepsilon>0$, some $\delta>0$ makes the number of integers $n<X$ satisfying $X^{-\delta}<P^+(n)/P^+(n+1)<X^\delta$ less than $\varepsilon X$ for all sufficiently large $X$ (Erdős and Pomerance 1978, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ErdosPomerance1978](#ref-ErdosPomerance1978)]. The lower-density bound was increased to $0.05544$ by de la Bretèche, Pomerance and Tenenbaum (de la Bretèche, Pomerance, and Tenenbaum 2005, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BretechePomeranceTenenbaum2005](#ref-BretechePomeranceTenenbaum2005)], and to $0.05866$ by an observation of Fouvry recorded in the same section. Wang (Wang 2017, 2018) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Wang2017](#ref-Wang2017), [Wang2018](#ref-Wang2018)] obtained $0.1063$ and $0.1356$, and Lü and Wang (Lü and Wang 2025) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[LuWang2025](#ref-LuWang2025)] obtained $0.2017$. Yang’s recent preprint (Yang 2026, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Yang2026](#ref-Yang2026)] gives $0.280$ for both orderings. These are bounds on lower natural densities; they do not assert that a natural density exists.

For the joint law itself, Teräväinen (Teräväinen 2018, Theorem 1.14) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Teravainen2018](#ref-Teravainen2018)] proved the predicted product in logarithmic density. That is, for fixed $0<a,b<1$, the indicator in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, averaged with weight $1/n$ and normalization $1/\log X$, tends to $\rho(1/a)\rho(1/b)$. His Theorem 1.16 gives logarithmic density $1/2$ for the ordering, while Theorem 1.19 proves positive lower natural density for every nondegenerate rectangle of normalized largest-prime-factor values inside $(0,1)^2$. Tao and Teräväinen (Tao and Teräväinen 2019, Remark 3.3, equation (50)) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[TaoTeravainen2019](#ref-TaoTeravainen2019)] then obtained the joint product law for ordinary averages outside an exceptional set of scales of logarithmic density zero; their Corollary 1.16 gives the corresponding ordering result. Wang (Wang 2021) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Wang2021Conditional](#ref-Wang2021Conditional)] proved the ordinary joint law under the Elliott–Halberstam conjecture for friable integers. Jiang, Lü and Wang (Jiang, Lü, and Wang 2022) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[JiangLuWang2022](#ref-JiangLuWang2022)] established averaged-over-shift forms of the conjectures, a different conclusion from a result at the fixed shift one.

The more recent work of Tao and Teräväinen (Tao and Teräväinen 2026, Theorem 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[TaoTeravainen2026Quantitative](#ref-TaoTeravainen2026Quantitative)] gives a quantitative joint law outside an exceptional set of scales, with uniformity in the smoothness parameters and a power saving in $\log X$. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> concerns fixed parameters and has no exceptional scales; it does not assert their quantitative error term or their uniformity for growing parameters. Relative to the results just described, the distinction is therefore the unconditional ordinary limit at every sufficiently large scale, not a new marginal distribution or a logarithmically averaged independence statement.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Method and organization

We first replace the largest prime factor by finitely many prime counts. For a fixed integer $J\ge2$, divide the primes in $(x^{1/J},x]$ into bins $\mathcal B_{k,x}=(x^{k/J},x^{(k+1)/J}]$, $1\le k<J$. If $\Omega_E(n)$ counts prime factors in $E$ with multiplicity, a choice of unit complex numbers $\zeta_k$ defines the bin character $$f_x(n)=\prod_{k=1}^{J-1}\zeta_k^{\Omega_{\mathcal B_{k,x}}(n)}.$$ Let $g_x$ be defined by a second arbitrary phase vector, and let $F_x=f_x-\mu$, where $\mu$ is the limiting mean of $f_x$. Finite Fourier inversion reduces the joint law to the mixed decorrelation $$\frac1x\sum_{n<x}\overline{g_x(n)}F_x(n+1)\longrightarrow0.$$ Here $x$ runs through integer scales. The two phase vectors may differ. This freedom gives independence of the two count vectors.

Two properties of these functions connect the beginning and end of the proof. First, every fixed positive integer multiplier has all its prime factors below the bins once $x$ is large. Thus $F_x(un)=F_x(n)$ and $g_x(un)=g_x(n)$ for each fixed $u$. Second, $F_x$ has cancellation in averages over growing intervals that remain short relative to the original scale. Let $B$ be the auxiliary parameter and let $T=T(B)\to\infty$ be the growing shift scale. If $P_B$ is a finite set of primes with $\min P_B\to\infty$, define $$G_{B,v}(n)=\prod_{\substack{p\in P_B\\p\mid n}}
                 \frac{1+p^{-v/B}}2,\qquad v\ge0.$$ For any interval $I_B$ of $L_B\to\infty$ consecutive integer shifts, and fixed $0<s_0<s_1$, positive integer $D$, residue $r\pmod D$, and $v\ge0$, Section <a href="#sec:labels" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:labels">2</a> proves $$\lim_{B\to\infty}\limsup_{x\to\infty}
 \frac1{Tx}\sum_{s_0Tx\le n\le s_1Tx}
 \left|\frac1{L_B}
   \sum_{\substack{i\in I_B\\i\equiv r\pmod D}}
     F_x(n+i)G_{B,v}(n+i)\right|^2=0.$$ At fixed $B$ the shift interval is short relative to $x$, while the base points have size $Tx$. This is the scale produced by the divisor substitution below.

The centered function $F_x$ is not multiplicative. On the relevant ranges $n=O_B(x)$, the bin counts take values in a fixed finite set. This permits interpolation by a fixed finite family of real, nonnegative multiplicative functions. After resolving the residue condition by Dirichlet characters, the real short-interval theorem of Matomäki and Radziwiłł (Matomäki and Radziwiłł 2016, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[MatomakiRadziwill2016](#ref-MatomakiRadziwill2016)] compares the principal components with long means whose centered combination vanishes. The complex theorem of Matomäki, Radziwiłł and Tao (Matomäki, Radziwiłł, and Tao 2015, Theorem A.1, corrected version) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[MatomakiRadziwillTao2015](#ref-MatomakiRadziwillTao2015)] controls the nonprincipal twists. The rest of the proof must reduce the mixed correlation to this particular cancellation statement.

Suppose, then, that the mixed correlation stays nonzero along a sequence of scales. The profinite integers $\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ encode compatible residue classes modulo all positive integers and carry Haar probability measure. Passing to a subsequence gives a bounded profile $W(t,w)$ on $(0,\infty)\times\widehat{\mathbb Z}$. Its integral against each fixed compactly supported continuous test $\Phi(t,w)$ is the subsequential limit of the mixed averages weighted by $\Phi(n/x,n)$, with $n$ in the second argument viewed through its residues. A fixed nonnegative compactly supported smooth cutoff $\phi$ can be chosen so that $\int\phi(t)W(t,w)\,dt\,dw\ne0$, where $dt$ is Lebesgue measure and $dw$ is Haar measure.

For an integer $n$, the amplifier defines $D_B(n)$ as a nonnegative weighted count of factorizations $n=am$ and $n+1=cl_a$, restricted by $e^B<c<e^{2B}$ and $Tc<a<2Tc$. At fixed $B$ its coefficient list is finite, and the same formula defines a function on $\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ depending only on residues at primes above a cutoff tending to infinity. The construction gives $D_B$ Haar mean at least $d_0>0$ for large $B$ and bounded $L^2$ norm. To prove these bounds, the argument passes to a comparison model in which each site prime is assigned by an independent fair coin to the coefficient divisor or the remaining factor. The first and second moments are analyzed through one split and two conditionally independent splits of the same site prime sets. For each fixed $B$, $\Phi(t,w)=\phi(t)D_B(w)$ is an allowed profile test, so along the selected subsequence $$\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac1x\sum_{n\ge1}
   \phi(n/x)\overline{g_x(n)}F_x(n+1)D_B(n)
 =\int\phi(t)W(t,w)D_B(w)\,dt\,dw.$$ Independence from every fixed residue coordinate and $L^2$ approximation of $\phi W$ show that the right-hand side has absolute value bounded away from zero as $B\to\infty$.

For fixed $B$ and large $x$, writing $n=am$ in this weighted average replaces $g_x(n)$ by $g_x(m)$. For each fixed pair $(c,m)$, the remaining terms form a sum over $a$ satisfying $c\mid am+1$, with nonnegative divisor and smooth weights multiplying $F_x(am+1)$. Cauchy–Schwarz in $(c,m)$ removes the unit-modulus factor $g_x(m)$ and squares this inner sum. The resulting normalized quadratic energy has a positive lower bound, while its equal-coefficient diagonal tends to zero in the same iterated limit. A positive contribution therefore remains from distinct indices $a,b$. Section <a href="#sec:amplification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:amplification">4</a> proves these moment and energy claims.

For such a pair, $c$ divides both $am+1$ and $bm+1$. Thus $m$ is a unit modulo $c$ and $a-b=jc$, with $0<|j|<T$ by the coefficient windows. Writing $l_a=(am+1)/c$ and $l_b=(bm+1)/c$, the exact substitution $$n'=b l_a,\qquad n'+j=a l_b$$ turns the label product into $\overline{F_x(n')}F_x(n'+j)$ by invariance under the fixed multipliers $a,b,c$. The new endpoints have size $Tx$. The off-diagonal energy is therefore a weighted graph of ordinary additive shifts. Its edge weights depend on endpoint prime sets and on additional residue data from each divisor representation.

Group the new endpoints into blocks $N+i$, $1\le i\le M$, with $M=\lceil C_6T\rceil$ for a fixed large $C_6$, and put $s=N/(Tx)$.
